\rho,\rho^{\prime}
, it is easy to check that
\rho(\Xi)-\rho^{\prime}(\Xi)
lies in
P_{<2g}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon},\mathbb{Q})
. By Lemma
8.6
,
\pi_{*}(\rho(\Xi))\in H^{*}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n},\mathbb{Q})
is independent of the choice of universal line bundle.

From Section
2.2
, compactified Jacobians admit a map to the logarithmic Picard group. For nondegenerate stability
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
, consider a fiber diagram (see also
[
2
, Section 6]
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{J}^{\epsilon_{1}}_{g,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LogPic}_{g,n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}

Let
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
be realization maps for
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
.
Theorem
1.1
(b) raises the following question:

Question 8.8
.

Let
\Xi=\Theta^{\ell}(\kappa_{0,1})^{m}\xi_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots\xi_{n}^{k_{n}}\in\mathrm{PicS}_{g,n}
. If
2\ell+m+\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\leq 2g
, does the difference
\rho_{2}(\Xi)-(h_{1})_{*}(h_{2})^{*}(\rho_{1}(\Xi))
lie in
P_{<2g}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon_{2}},\mathbb{Q})
?

A positive answer to the question of the perversity of the wall-crossing term will provide a conceptual explanation of Theorem
1.1
(b) in rational cohomology. For arbitrary
\alpha\in\mathrm{PicS}_{g,n}
it is an interesting question to find
k
such that
\rho(\alpha)\in P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon},\mathbb{Q})\setminus P_{k-1}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\epsilon},\mathbb{Q})
.

We thank Davesh Maulik, Miguel Moreira, and Qizheng Yin, whose comments led to the formulation of the following remark.

Remark 8.9
.

By
[
41
, Theorem 0.1]
, or through a mild generalization using
[
44
]
, the perverse filtration associated with
\pi
is multiplicative. Therefore the cup product descends to the associated graded space
\mathrm{gr}_{P}^{\,\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\,\epsilon},\mathbb{Q})
. Under the Poincaré duality, Theorem
1.1
(b) raises the question of whether the ring structure on
\mathrm{gr}_{P}^{\,\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\,\epsilon},\mathbb{Q})
is independent on the stability condition. It would be interesting to seek an explanation for such independence.

9
Weight decomposition and Fourier transforms

9.1
Weight decomposition

We discuss the generalization of Beauville
[
12
]
and Deninger-Murre
[
22
]
’s weight decomposition for semi-abelian schemes. Any semi-abelian variety
G
can be written as an extension

0\to T\to G\to A\to 0

of an abelian variety
A
by a split torus
T
. For a semi-abelian scheme
\pi:G\to B
, the
Kimura dimension
of
\pi
is defined by

\mathrm{kd}(\pi):=\max\{2\dim(A_{b})+\dim(T_{b}):b\in B\}\,.

(95)

For
N\in\mathbb{Z}
, consider the “multiplication by
N
map”

[N]:G\to G\,.

(96)

The weight
w
piece is defined by

\mathsf{CH}_{(w)}^{*}(G):=\{\alpha\in\mathsf{CH}^{*}(G,\mathbb{Q}):[N]^{*}\alpha=N^{w}\alpha,\text{ for all }N\in\mathbb{Z}\}\,.

(97)

The multiplicative splitting is proven by Ancona-Huber-Pepin Lehalleur
[
4
, Theorem 4.9]
.

Theorem 9.1

(
[
4
]
)
.

The rational Chow group of
G
has the multiplicative decomposition

\mathsf{CH}^{*}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C},\mathbb{Q})=\bigoplus_{w=0}^{\mathrm{kd}(\pi)}\mathsf{CH}^{*}_{(w)}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C})\,.

In particular, if the weight of a class is greater than the Kimura dimension
\mathrm{kd}(\pi)
, it vanishes.

9.2
Leading term of partial Fourier transform

In Section
8
, we studied the connection between
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}
(
50
) and
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}
both from
\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\,\epsilon})
to
\mathsf{CH}^{*}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C})
. Here, we compute the leading term of
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}(1)
using Theorem
9.1
.
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}
has a nice compatibility property with the “multiplication by
N
” map (
96
).

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
x\in\mathsf{CH}^{p}(\overline{J}_{C},\mathbb{Q})
. If we write
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}(x)=\sum_{q}y_{q}
with
y_{q}\in\mathsf{CH}^{q}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C},\mathbb{Q})
, then

[N]^{*}y_{q}=N^{g+q-p}y_{q},\,N\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Proof.

By (
18
), we have

(1\times[N])^{*}c_{1}(\mathcal{P})=N\cdot c_{1}(\mathcal{P})\,.

(98)

Then the result follows from the projection formula. See
[
12
]
and
[
22
, Proposition 2.16]
.
∎

We present the main calculation of this section.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
B=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
and
\pi:J^{\underline{0}}_{C}\to B
be the relative Jacobian. Then we have

[\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(1)]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}=\frac{\Theta^{g}}{g!}\in\mathsf{CH}^{g}(J^{\underline{0}}_{g},\mathbb{Q})\,.

Proof.

We first compute
\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(\exp(-\Theta))
. By Lemma
5.10
and the projection formula, we have

\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(\exp(-\Theta))=\exp(\Theta)\cdot p_{2*}\mu^{*}\exp(-\Theta)\,.

(99)

Let
\partial B:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g-1,n+2}
and
j:\partial B\to B
be the gluing map. We also denote
j:J^{\underline{0}}_{C}|_{\partial B}\to J^{\underline{0}}_{C}
the associated finite morphism. If
w>2g
, we have
\mathsf{CH}^{*}_{(w)}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C})=0
by Theorem
9.1
. For
\partial\overline{J}_{C}:=\overline{J}_{C}\setminus J^{\underline{0}}_{C}
, we consider the excision sequence

\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\partial\overline{J}_{C},\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{j_{*}}\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\overline{J}_{C},\mathbb{Q})\to\mathsf{CH}^{*}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C},\mathbb{Q})\to 0\,.

Since
\Theta
has weight
2
, if
c>g
, then
\Theta^{c}
restricted to
J^{\underline{0}}_{C}
vanishes. By the above excision sequence,
\Theta^{c}
is supported on
\partial\overline{J}_{C}
. The pullback of (
8
) to
\partial\overline{J}_{C}
yields

\mu:\partial\overline{J}_{C}\times_{\partial B}J^{\underline{0}}_{C}|_{\partial B}\to\partial\overline{J}_{C}\,.

If
c>g
, using the projection formula, the class
p_{2*}\mu^{*}(\Theta^{c})=0
lies in the image of
j_{*}
. Therefore, (
99
) can be written by

\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{g}(-\exp(\Theta))=\exp(\Theta)\cdot(1+\mathrm{Im}\,j_{*})\,.

(100)

By Theorem
9.1
, one can take the pure weight pieces of (
99
). Taking the codimension
g
part of (
100
), we get

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(\frac{(-\Theta)^{k}}{k!})\Big]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}=\frac{\Theta^{g}}{g!}+\big[\mathrm{Im}\,j_{*}\big]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}\,.

By Lemma
9.2
, we have

\big[\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(\Theta^{k})\big]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}\in\mathsf{CH}^{g}_{(2g-k)}(J^{\underline{0}}_{C})\,.

The weight
2g
part of the left hand side is
[\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(1)]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}
. Since the Kimura dimension (
95
) of the boundary
J^{\underline{0}}_{C}|_{\partial B}
drops to
2g-1
, the weight
2g
piece of
\mathrm{Im}\,j_{*}
vanishes by Theorem
9.1
. Therefore the weight
2g
part of the right hand side is
\frac{\Theta^{g}}{g!}
and we get the equality.
∎

9.3
Universal double ramification cycle formula over the treelike locus

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
be the locus of
treelike
curves–these are stable curves
whose graph is a tree with any number of self-loops attached, which is an open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
. It can be uniquely characterized as the largest open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
where every Abel-Jacobi section extends
[
34
]
. We give a proof of the universal double ramification formula (Proposition
8.2
) on
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
, independent from
[
8
]
. We use compatibility of Fourier transform and the group structure of
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}
.

Over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
, the compactified Jacobians are isomorphic to the relative moduli space of multidegree zero, rank 1 torsion free sheaves, and we denote the compactified Jacobian of degree
0
by
\pi:\overline{J}^{\text{\,tl}}_{g,n}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
. For
b\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=(2g-2+n)b
, the twisted
\Theta
-divisor is defined by

\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}}:=\Theta-\frac{b}{2}\kappa_{0,1}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\psi_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\xi_{i}+\pi^{*}D_{b;\mathsf{a}}\,,

(101)

Here
D_{b;\mathsf{a}}
is a divisor class given by

D_{b;\mathsf{a}}=\sum_{\Gamma}c(\Gamma,b;\mathsf{a})[\Gamma]\,,

where the sum is over all stable graphs
\Gamma
with exactly two vertices connected
by a single edge described by partition
(g_{1},I_{1}|g_{2},I_{2})
and
c(\Gamma,b;\mathsf{a})=-(\sum_{i\in I_{1}}a_{i}-b(2g_{1}-2+|I_{1}|+1))^{2}
. We refer
[
8
, Section 4.1]
for the further explanations.

The combinatorics of the universal double ramification formula (
72
) gets simplified over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
. For
h>0
, let
j_{h}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g-\ell,n;2h}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
be the restriction of the gluing map to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
. In general,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g-h,n;2h}
is an open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g-h,n+2h}
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
be the relative Jacobian. For
b\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=(2g-2+n)b
, let
\mathsf{P}^{c}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})
be the universal double ramification formula (
72
) restricted to
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}
.

(a)

For all
c
, we have

\mathsf{P}^{c}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})=\left[\exp(\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}})\cdot\sum_{h=0}^{g}\frac{(-1)^{h}}{2^{h}h!}(j_{h})_{*}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\left(\sum_{k_{i}=1}^{\infty}\frac{B_{2k_{i}}}{k_{i}!}\frac{(\psi_{h_{i}}+\psi_{h_{i}^{\prime}})^{k_{i}-1}}{2^{k_{i}}}\right)\right]_{\text{codim}=c}\in\mathsf{CH}^{c}(J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n})\,.

(b)

For all
c,
we have
\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{c}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})=\frac{(\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}})^{c}}{c!}
.

Proof.

We apply
[
8
, Proposition 4.1]
. The divisor inside the exponential in
[
8
, eq (4.2)]
coincides with (
101
). Both (a) and (b) follows from Faulhaber’s formula applied to the contribution from the
r
-weights.
∎

We replace Theorem
8.3
by the below:

Proposition 9.5
.

For any
b\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=(2g-2+n)b
, suppose that we have
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{h}(\exp(-b\kappa_{0,1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\xi_{i}))=\mathsf{P}_{h}^{h}(b;\mathsf{a})
for all genus
h
less than
g
. Let
\Xi\in\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\overline{J}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{h,n})
be any monomial of
\xi
and
\kappa_{0,1}
-classes. For any genus
h
less than
g
, we have

(a)

\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h}(\Xi)\in\mathsf{CH}^{\geq h}(J^{\underline{0}}_{h,n})
, and

(b)

[(-1)^{h}\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h}(\exp(-b\kappa_{0,1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\xi_{i}))]_{\mathrm{codim}=h}=\frac{(\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}})^{h}}{h!}

Proof.

We first prove (a) by induction on
h
. Suppose the statement holds for all genera less than
h
. By assumption, we have
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{h}(\exp(-b\kappa_{0,1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\xi_{i}))=\mathsf{P}^{h}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})\in\mathsf{CH}^{h}(J^{\underline{0}}_{h,n})
.
From Lemma
9.4
(a) the polynomiality of
\mathsf{P}_{h}^{h}(b;\mathsf{a})
in
b,\mathsf{a}
follows from the polynomiality of (
101
). Therefore, for any monomial
\Xi
of
\xi
and
\kappa_{0,1}
-classes, we have
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{h}(\Xi)\in\mathsf{CH}^{h}(J^{\underline{0}}_{h})
. By Proposition
4.6
and Proposition
5.9
, we get

\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{h}(\Xi)=(-1)^{h}\mathfrak{F}_{h}^{\circ}(\Xi)+\sum_{f=1}^{h}\frac{1}{2^{f}f!}(j_{f})_{*}(-1)^{h-f}\mathfrak{F}_{h-f}^{\circ}\Big(\Xi\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{f}\sum_{k_{i}=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{k_{i}}B_{2k_{i}}}{(2k_{i})!}\frac{\alpha_{i}^{2k_{i}-1}+\beta_{i}^{2k_{i}-1}}{\alpha_{i}+\beta_{i}}\Big)\,.

(102)

where
\alpha_{i}=\psi_{n+i}-\xi_{n+i}+\xi_{n+f+i}
and
\beta_{i}=\psi_{n+f+i}+\xi_{n+i}-\xi_{n+f+i}
. The term inside
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h-f}
is a polynomial in
\psi_{i},\xi_{i}
and
\kappa_{0,1}
-classes. By linearity of
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{g-h}
,
\psi
-classes can be factored out. By the induction hypothesis, these terms has codimension at least
h-g
, so
(j_{f})_{*}\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h-f}(\cdots)
has codimension at least
h
. Therefore, we conclude that
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h}(\Xi)\in\mathsf{CH}^{\geq h}(J^{\underline{0}}_{h,n})
.

Similarly, we prove (b) by induction on
h
. Suppose the statement holds for all genera less than
h
. In (
102
), we substitute
\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}}:=\exp(-b\kappa_{0,1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\xi_{i})
:

\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{h}(\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}})=(-1)^{h}\mathfrak{F}_{h}^{\circ}(\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}})+\sum_{f=1}^{h}\frac{1}{2^{f}f!}(j_{f})_{*}(-1)^{h-f}\mathfrak{F}_{h-f}^{\circ}\Big(\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{f}\sum_{k_{i}=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{k_{i}}B_{2k_{i}}}{(2k_{i})!}\frac{\alpha_{i}^{2k_{i}-1}+\beta_{i}^{2k_{i}-1}}{\alpha_{i}+\beta_{i}}\Big)\,.

(103)

Take codimension
h
part of each term in (
103
). By (a), within
\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h-f}(\cdots)
, only pure monomials of
\xi
and
\kappa_{0,1}
classes will contribute. By the induction hypothesis, the boundary term takes the form

\big[(-1)^{h-f}\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h-f}\Big(\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{f}(\xi_{h_{i}}-\xi_{h_{i}^{\prime}})^{2k_{i}-2}\Big)\big]_{\mathrm{codim}=h-f}=\frac{(\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}})^{h-k}}{(h-k)!}\prod_{i=1}^{f}\frac{(2k_{i}-1)!}{(k_{i}-1)!}\Big(\frac{\psi_{h_{i}}+\psi_{h_{i}^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)^{k_{i}-1}

modulo relations
\xi_{h_{1}}-\xi_{h_{1}^{\prime}}=\cdots=\xi_{h_{f}}-\xi_{h_{f}^{\prime}}=0
. Here,
k:=\sum_{i}k_{i}
and the relations are from Lemma
2.3
. By Lemma
9.4
(a), the boundary term of the right hand side (
103
) aligns, leading to
[(-1)^{h}\mathfrak{F}^{\circ}_{h}(\Xi_{b;\mathsf{a}})]_{\mathrm{codim}=h}=\frac{(\Theta_{b;\mathsf{a}})^{h}}{h!}
.
∎

We prove the main result of this section. Over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
, there exists a unique piecewise linear function
\alpha
on the universal curve such that the line bundle
\omega_{C,\log}^{\otimes-b}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}x_{i})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(\alpha)
has multidegree zero for all underlying curves in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
. After twisting by
\alpha
, the Abel-Jacobi section
\mathsf{aj}_{b;\mathsf{a}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}\to\overline{J}^{\,\text{tl}}_{g,n}
is well-defined and factors through
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}
.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
be the relative Jacobian. For any
b\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=(2g-2+n)b
, we have

[\mathsf{aj}_{b;\mathsf{a}}]=\mathsf{P}^{g}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})\in\mathsf{CH}^{g}(J^{\underline{0}}_{g,n})\,.

(104)

Proof.

We prove this by induction on the genus. For
g=0
the statement is trivial. Suppose we know (
104
) for genus
h
less than
g
. We first prove (
104
) for
g=0
and
\mathsf{a}=0
. By Proposition
5.2
, we get
[e]=\mathfrak{F}^{-1}_{g}(1)
.
By Proposition
9.5
(a), after taking the codimension
g
part, we get

[e]=[(-1)^{g}\mathfrak{F}_{g}^{\circ}(1)]_{\mathrm{codim}=g}+\sum_{h=1}^{g}\frac{1}{2^{h}h!}(j_{h})_{*}\Big[(-1)^{g-h}\mathfrak{F}_{g-h}^{\circ}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{k_{i}=1}^{\infty}\frac{B_{2k_{i}}(2k_{i}-1)}{(2k_{i})!}(\xi_{h_{i}}-\xi_{h_{i}}^{\prime})^{2k_{i}-2}\Big)\Big]_{\mathrm{codim}=g-h}\,.

By Proposition
9.5
(b), the right hand side matches with Lemma
9.4
. This finishes the proof for genus
g
with
\mathsf{a}=0
.

We prove for arbitrary
b\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=(2g-2+n)b
. Let
\alpha
be the unique piecewise linear function on the universal curve necessary to construct the Abel-Jacobi section. Then, the Abel-Jacobi section can be written as

\mathsf{aj}_{b;\mathsf{a}}=\widetilde{\phi}_{b;\mathsf{a}}\circ e:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}\to\overline{J}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}\,,

where
e:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}\to\overline{J}_{g,n}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}
is the unit section and
\widetilde{\phi}_{b;\mathsf{a}}
is an automorphism of
\overline{J}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
induced by
L\mapsto L\otimes\omega_{C,\log}^{\otimes-b}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}x_{i})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(\alpha)
.
By (
101
) and Lemma
9.4
, we have
\widetilde{\phi}_{-b;-\mathsf{a}}^{*}\mathsf{P}^{c}_{g}(0;0)=\mathsf{P}^{c}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})
. Therefore, by twisting (
104
) for
\mathsf{a}=0
with respect to
\widetilde{\phi}_{b;\mathsf{a}}
, we have

[\mathsf{aj}_{b;\mathsf{a}}]=\widetilde{\phi}^{*}_{-b;-\mathsf{a}}[e]=\widetilde{\phi}^{*}_{-b;-\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{P}^{g}_{g}(0;0)=\mathsf{P}^{g}_{g}(b;\mathsf{a})\,.

Thus we obtain the result from the induction.
∎

By the argument in Section
8.2
, Theorem
9.6
gives an independent proof of Theorem
1.1
over
B=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\mathrm{tl}}_{g,n}
for
\ell=0
.

9.4
Fourier transform for principally polarized abelian schemes

We extend our argument in the previous sections to abelian schemes. Let
\pi:A\to B
be a principally polarized abelian scheme (p.p.a.s.) of relative dimension
g
over a smooth scheme
B
. Let
e:B\to A
be the unit section and let
\mu:A\times_{B}A\to A
be the addition. For
n\geq 1
, we denote

A^{n}:=A\times_{B}\cdots\times_{B}A\text{\hskip 5.69054pt ($n$-times)}\,,

and
A^{0}:=B
. For
1\leq i\leq n
, let
p_{i}:A^{n}\to A
be the
i
-th projection and for
1\leq i<j\leq n
, let
p_{ij}:A^{n}\to A\times_{B}A
be the projection to
i,j
-th factor. Let
\lambda_{m}:=c_{m}(\mathbb{E})\in\mathsf{CH}^{m}(B)
be the
m
-th Chern class of the Hodge bundle
\mathbb{E}:=\pi_{*}\Omega_{\pi}
.

For an abelian scheme
A\to B
, let
A^{\vee}:=\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{A/B}
denote the dual abelian scheme and let
\mathcal{P}
be the Poincaré line bundle on
A\times_{B}A^{\vee}
which is trivialized along the unit sections. Assume that
A\to B
is principally polarized; that is, there exists a polarization
\lambda:A\to A^{\vee}
which is an isomorphism. We identify
(\mathrm{id},\lambda):A\times_{B}A\xrightarrow{\cong}A\times_{B}A^{\vee}
. Consider a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle

L_{\lambda}:=((e,\lambda)^{*}\mathcal{P})^{-1/2}\in\mathrm{Pic}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

which is relatively ample, symmetric and trivialized along the unit section.
Let
\Theta:=c_{1}(L_{\lambda})\in\mathsf{CH}^{1}(A)
. For
1\leq i\leq n
, let
\Theta_{i}:=p_{i}^{*}\Theta
and for
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
, let
\ell_{ij}:=p_{ij}^{*}c_{1}(\mathcal{P})
in
\mathsf{CH}^{1}(A^{n})
.

Definition 9.7
.

The tautological ring
\mathsf{R}^{*}(A^{n})
is the subring of
\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A^{n},\mathbb{Q})
generated by classes

\{\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n},\,\{\ell_{ij}\}_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n},\,\{\lambda_{m}\}_{1\leq m\leq g}\,.

For
n\geq 1
, we consider a p.p.a.s.
\pi:A^{n}\to A^{n-1}
where
\pi
is the projection along the first
n-1
factors. Under the weight decomposition (
97
), the weights of tautological classes are given by

\Theta_{n}\in\mathsf{CH}^{1}_{(2)}(A^{n}),\text{ and }\,\ell_{i,n}\in\mathsf{CH}^{1}_{(1)}(A^{n}),1\leq i\leq n-1\,.

(105)

The first follows from the fact that
\Theta_{n}
is a symmetric ample class and the second follows from the Theorem of Square. Other generators of tautological ring has weight zero. Since the weight is multiplicative, (
105
) determines the weight of all tautological classes.

We consider sections of
\pi:A^{n}\to A^{n-1}
induced from the group structure.
Let
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
be a vector of integers. We define the
\pi
-relative
translation automorphism
by

\tau_{\mathsf{a}}:A^{n}\to A^{n},(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\mapsto(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1},x_{n}+a_{1}x_{1}+\cdots+a_{n-1}x_{n-1})\,.

(106)

For
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
, the
translation section
is defined by
\sigma_{\mathsf{a}}:=\tau_{\mathsf{a}}\circ e:A^{n-1}\to A^{n}
.

Lemma 9.8
.

For
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
, we have
\tau_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\Theta_{n})=\Theta_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\ell_{in}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}^{2}\Theta_{i}
.

Proof.

We write (
106
) as follows. Consider the composition
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}:A^{n}\xrightarrow{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1},1)}A^{n}\xrightarrow{\mu_{n}}A
where the first morphism is the “multiplication by
N
” map with the prescribed multiplicity and
\mu_{n}
is the addition. For
\mu:A\times_{B}A\to A
, we have the Mumford formula

\mu^{*}(\Theta)=-\ell_{12}+p_{1}^{*}\Theta+p_{2}^{*}\Theta\,.

(107)

For the morphism
\mu\times\mathrm{id}:A\times_{B}A\times_{B}A\to A\times_{B}A
, we have
(\mu\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\ell_{12}=\ell_{13}+\ell_{23}
by the theorem of the cube. Therefore we get
\mu_{n}^{*}(\Theta)=\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\ell_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Theta_{i}
.

From the weight of divisors (
105
), the pullback of tautological classes along the map
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1},1)
is clear. Since
\tau_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\Theta)=\tau_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}p_{n}^{*}(\Theta)=\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{*}(\Theta)
, the result follows.
∎

We consider the Fourier-Mukai transform (
[
51
,
22
]
)

\mathfrak{F}:=\mathrm{ch}(\mathcal{P})=\exp(\ell_{nn+1}):\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A^{n})\to\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A^{n})\,.

(108)

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
be a vector of integers. Then we have

\mathfrak{F}^{-1}\big(\exp(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\ell_{in})\big)=\frac{\big(\Theta_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\ell_{in}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}^{2}\Theta_{i}\big)^{g}}{g!}\,.

(109)

Proof.

By the argument in Proposition
9.3
, we have
\mathfrak{F}(\exp(\Theta_{n}))=\exp(-\Theta_{n})
. By Proposition
5.2
and
[
22
, Lemma 2.18]
, we have

[e]=\mathfrak{F}^{-1}(1)=\frac{(\Theta_{n})^{g}}{g!}\,.

(110)

The pullback of (
110
) along (
106
) follows from Lemma
9.8
. By the argument in the proof of Theorem
9.6
, we get the result.
∎

Theorem 9.10
.

The Fourier-Mukai transform (
108
) preserves tautological classes. Moreover, proper pushforward along the projection
\pi:A^{n}\to A^{n-1}
preserves tautological classes.

Proof.

Let
\alpha:=(\Theta_{n})^{m}(\ell_{1n-1})^{k_{1}}\cdots(\ell_{n-1n})^{k_{n}}\in\mathsf{R}^{*}(A^{n})
. Since
\mathfrak{F}
is linear over the base, it suffices to check that the image
\mathfrak{F}(\alpha)
is tautological. For simplicity, we write
k=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}k_{i}
. If
m+k\leq g
, by Proposition
9.9
, we have
\mathfrak{F}(\alpha)\in\mathsf{R}^{*}(A^{n})
.

We assume
m+k>g
. The class
\alpha
has weight
2m+k
. By weight vanishing part of Theorem
9.1
, we may assume
2m+k\leq 2g
. Additionally, since
\Theta_{n}\in\mathsf{CH}_{(2)}^{1}(A^{n})
, we have
(\Theta_{n})^{c}=0
for
c>g
. For
\mathsf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
, we consider the translation automorphism (
106
). By Lemma
9.8
, for
c>g
, we have:

\big(\Theta_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\ell_{in}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}^{2}\Theta_{i}\big)^{c}=0\,.

(111)

Using the polynomiality of the relation (
111
) in
a_{i}
’s we can further decompose (
111
). Therefore the class
\alpha
can be written as a momomial
\alpha^{\prime}
of
\Theta_{n}
and
\ell_{in}
classes of degree less than or equal to
g
, multiplied by classes that are polynomial in
\Theta_{1},\ldots,\Theta_{n-1}
. As
\mathfrak{F}
is linear over the base, we have reduced to the case when
m+k\leq g
. Therefore
\mathfrak{F}
preserves tautological classes.

By using a similar argument as in Proposition
8.1
, we find
\pi_{*}(\alpha)=e^{*}\mathfrak{F}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A_{n})
. Clearly
e^{*}:\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A^{n})\to\mathsf{CH}^{*}(A^{n-1})
preserves tautological classes. Thus, by the argument above, the pushforward along
\pi:A^{n}\to A^{n-1}
preserves tautological classes.
∎

By the argument in Theorem
9.10
, we get a closed formula for
\pi_{*}:\mathsf{R}^{*}(A^{n})\to\mathsf{R}^{*}(A^{n-1})
.

Corollary 9.11
.

Let
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. If all
k_{i}
are even and
2m+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}k_{i}=2g
, then we have

\pi_{*}\big(\frac{(\Theta_{n})^{m}}{m!}\frac{(\ell_{1n})^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\cdots\frac{(\ell_{n-1n})^{k_{n-1}}}{k_{n-1}!}\big)=(-1)^{g-m}\frac{\Theta_{1}^{k_{1}/2}}{(k_{1}/2)!}\cdots\frac{\Theta_{n-1}^{k_{n-1}/2}}{(k_{n-1}/2)!}\,.

Otherwise, the pushforward is zero.

9.5
Torus rank at most one locus

Let
\mathcal{A}_{g}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
be the canonical partial compactification of rank
1
degenerations
[
52
]
. Let
\pi:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
be the universal family and let
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
be the universal semi-abelian scheme. We adapt the argument from the previous sections to study the intersection theory of this family. Let
\mu:\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}
be the natural action. Then the tuple

(\pi:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g},\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g},\mu:\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g})

(112)

is a
degenerate abelian scheme
in the sense of Arinkin-Fedorov
[
7
, Definition 2.1]
.

We show that the auto-equivalence of
\mathcal{X}_{g}
extends to
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}
. Let
\Theta
be the principal polarization of
\mathcal{X}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}_{g}
which is trivialized along the unit section. By slight abuse of notation, let
\Theta
also denote a relatively ample class on
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}
that extends this polarization
[
3
]
. Following
[
7
]
, the Poincaré line bundle
\mathcal{P}
on
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}
defined by

\mathcal{P}=\mu^{*}\Theta\otimes p_{1}^{*}\Theta^{\vee}\otimes p_{2}^{*}\Theta^{\vee}

(113)

which extends to a line bundle
\mathcal{P}
on
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}
. For compactified Jacobians, the failure to extending the Poincaré line bundle to the product
\overline{J}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}_{C}
was attributed to the lack of a common universal curve on the two factors. For
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
, the failure to extend
\mathcal{P}
is that the multiplication map
\mu
does not extend to
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
.

We can resolve the indeterminacy of the action
\mu
for any toroidal compactification of
\mathcal{X}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}_{g}
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\text{trop}}_{g}:=\textup{Sym}_{rc}^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{g})/\textup{GL}_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
be the moduli space of principally polarized tropical abelian varieties and let
\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\text{trop}}_{g}
be the the universal tropical abelian variety. Any toroidal compactification
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}\to\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{g}
corresponds to a subdivision
\Sigma_{A}
of
\mathcal{A}^{\text{trop}}_{g}
and a subdivision
\Sigma_{X}
of
\Sigma_{A}|_{\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}}
. Let

\mu:\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\text{trop}}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}\to\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}

be the multiplication map. Let
\Sigma_{X}^{\mu}\to\Sigma_{X}
be the base change of
\mu:\Sigma_{X}\times_{\Sigma_{A}}\Sigma_{X}\to\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}|_{\Sigma_{A}}
along
\Sigma_{X}\to\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}|_{\Sigma_{A}}
.
This subdivision defines a log modification
f:\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\mu}_{g}\to\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{g}}\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}
together with a map
\mu:\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\mu}_{g}\to\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}
extending the multiplication map on
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}
.

We specialize to
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
. Inside any
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{g}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
sits as the inverse image of
\mathcal{A}_{g}\sqcup\mathcal{A}_{g-1}
from Satake compactification and
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
is the unique minimal family that contains the identity section in its smooth locus. The restriction of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\mu}_{g}
to the locus
\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}
is denoted by
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\mu}
.
The fan
\Sigma_{A}
restricts to the ray
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, and
\mathcal{X}^{\text{trop}}_{g}|_{\Sigma_{A}}
is the torus with fiber
\mathbb{R}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
over
\ell\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
(and a point over
0
). The subdivision
\Sigma_{X}
is the subdivision of
\mathbb{R}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
adding the lattice points
\ell\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore,
\Sigma_{X}^{\mu}
over the torus-rank
1
locus is the subdivision of the product
\mathbb{R}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\times_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{R}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
along the diagonal

x+y=0\,(\textup{mod}\ell\mathbb{Z})

By the same analysis as in Theorem
1.2
(a),
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
has ordinary double point singularities, and
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\mu}
is a small resolution, i.e. it is smooth and the complement of
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}}\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{g}
has codimension
2
. Moreover, nontrivial fibers of
f:\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\mu}\to\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
are
\mathbb{P}^{1}
s.

Proposition 9.12
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
be the unique line bundle on
\mathcal{X}^{\mu}_{g}
which extends
\mathcal{P}
. Then the degree of
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
on the exceptional fibers of
f:\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\mu}\to\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\prime}
is
1
.

Proof.

The exceptional locus of
f
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over the boundary of
\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\prime}
, so we check the degree on any given fiber. Under the Torelli map, it is enough to check the degree over a point corresponding to a
\overline{J}_{C}
where
C
is an integral curve with one one self-node. Two resolutions
\overline{J}_{C}^{(2)}
and the resolution
\overline{J}^{\mu}_{C}
given by
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\mu}
of
\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
corresponds to two different Atiyah flops of ordinary double point singularity. To compare two, we form the common refinement
\overline{J}^{b}_{C}
of
\overline{J}_{C}^{(2)}
and
\overline{J}_{C}^{\mu}
as follows. The product
\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
is locally modeled on the toric variety

\left\{(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime}):\ell_{e_{i}}^{\prime}+\ell_{e_{i}}^{\prime\prime}=\ell_{e}\right\}

and
\overline{J}_{C}^{b}
corresponds to the subdivision
\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})
.
Following Definition
3.3
and Definition
3.7
, the multiplication map on
\overline{J}_{C}^{b}
has the following modular description:

\mu(\widetilde{C}\to C_{i},L_{i})=L_{1}|_{\widetilde{C}}\otimes L_{2}|_{\widetilde{C}}(\alpha)

where
\alpha
is the unique piecewise linear function up to pullback from the base such that the line bundle
L_{1}|_{\widetilde{C}}\otimes L_{2}|_{\widetilde{C}}(\alpha)
is stable on
\widetilde{C}
.

We compute
\alpha
. Let
\Gamma
be the tropical curve associated to
C
which has a single vertex
v
with a loop, where we fix some orientation. The tropical curves
\Gamma_{i}
associated to
C_{i}
have one additional vertex
w_{i}
at distance
\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}
from
v
respectively, measured with respect to the given orientation; the tropical divisors associated to
L_{1},L_{2}
have degree
-1
at
v
and
1
at the exceptional vertex.
We look first at the cone of
\overline{J}_{C}^{b}
where
\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}
is minimal. We form the tropical curve which is the subdivision of
\Gamma
at a vertex
u
at distance
\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime}+\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}
from
v
in the given orientation. Since
\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}
is minimal,
\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime}+\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}<\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime}+\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime}=\ell_{e}
, and hence in the given orientation we have an oriented path
v,w_{2},w_{1},u,v
. We form the tropical
D_{u}
divisor with degree
-1
at
v
and
1
at
u
. Then, we have

D_{1}+D_{2}-D_{u}=\textup{div}(\alpha)

where
\alpha
has slope
1
from
v
to
w_{2}
, slope
1
from
u
to
w_{1}
, and slope
0
elsewhere
7
7

7

We are using here the convention that the multidegree of
\mathcal{O}(\alpha)
is
-\textup{div}(\alpha)
.
.
If we normalize
\alpha
so that
\alpha(v)=0
, it follows that
\alpha(w_{1})=\alpha(w_{2})=\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime}
, and
\alpha(u)=0
. A similar calculation at the other cones of
\overline{J}^{b}
shows that in general
\alpha(w_{1})=\alpha(w_{2})=\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})
.

We now compare two models of extended Poincaré line bundles. By the bilinearity of the Deligne pairing, we have

\mu^{*}\Theta-p_{1}^{*}\Theta-p_{2}^{*}\Theta=-c_{1}(\left\langle L_{1},L_{2}\right\rangle)-c_{1}(\left\langle L_{1},\mathcal{O}(\alpha)\right\rangle)-c_{1}(\left\langle L_{2},\mathcal{O}(\alpha)\right\rangle)-\frac{1}{2}c_{1}(\left\langle\mathcal{O}(\alpha),\mathcal{O}(\alpha)\right\rangle)\,.

(114)

Since
\alpha(w_{i})=\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})
, using Lemma
5.5
, the last three terms of (
114
) is

-\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})-\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})+\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})=-\min(\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime},\ell_{e_{1}}^{\prime\prime},\ell_{e_{2}}^{\prime\prime})

which is the negative of the class of the exceptional divisor
E
of
\overline{J}_{C}^{b}\to\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
. The degree of
-\left\langle L_{1},L_{2}\right\rangle
has been calculated to be
-1
on the exceptional
\mathbb{P}^{1}
s of
\overline{J}_{C}^{(2)}
in Theorem
1.2
. On the blowup of the ordinary double point the line bundle
\mu^{*}\Theta-p_{1}^{*}\Theta-p_{2}^{*}\Theta
is thus represented by
-E-D
, where
D
is the strict transform of one of the two Weil divisors that are not blown up in the construction of the flop
\overline{J}_{C}^{(2)}\to\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
. If
B
denotes the strict transform of one of the adjacent Weil divisors to
D
, the divisor
-E-D-B
is rationally equivalent to
0
8
8

8

for instance, if we take
(0,0,1),(1,0,1),(0,1,1),(1,1,1)
as the four vertices of the square,
D
the bottom right vertex,
B
the bottom left, then
-E-D-B
is the divisor of the character
e_{2}^{*}-e_{3}^{*}
, where
e_{i}^{*}
are the standard coordinates of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
.
; hence we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}=-E-D\sim B

on the exceptional fibers of
\overline{J}^{b}_{C}\to\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
. The divisor
B
does not get blown up in the complementary flop
\overline{J}^{\mu}_{C}\to\overline{J}_{C}\times\overline{J}_{C}
, and so descends to a Cartier divisor on
\overline{J}^{\mu}_{C}
, where it has degree
1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

By Proposition
9.12
and following the argument in Theorem
1.2
,
\overline{\mathcal{P}}:=Rf_{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
is a maximal Cohen-Macaulay sheaf extending
\mathcal{P}
. To show that
\overline{\mathcal{P}}
induces an auto-equivalence we follow
[
6
]
. This follows from the cohomology calculation in
[
7
, Theorem 1]
and the fact that
\pi:\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
is a
\delta
-regular family.
∎

We adapt (
33
). The boundary
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{g}
admits a natural two-to-one cover
i:\mathcal{X}_{g-1}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{g}
.
Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{g-1}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g-1}}\mathcal{X}_{g-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}|_{\mathcal{X}_{g-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{g-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

(115)

where
p_{1}
is the first projection.

If we restrict
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{g}
to the boundary
i
, then it is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor over
\mathcal{X}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g-1}}\mathcal{X}_{g-1}
. Let
q
be the projection from the torsor to
\mathcal{X}_{g}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{g-1}}\mathcal{X}_{g-1}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

From Theorem
1.5
, let
\mathfrak{F}:\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathsf{CH}^{*}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{g})
be the Fourier transform and
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}
be its inverse with the normalization as in (
40
) and (
41
). By the same argument in Proposition
5.2
, we get
[e]=\mathfrak{F}^{-1}(1)
. Let
\mathbb{E}:=\pi_{*}\Omega_{\pi,\log}^{1}
be the Hodge bundle. By
[
24
]
,
c(\mathbb{E})c(\mathbb{E}^{\vee})=1
still holds. The Todd class of the residue sheaf
\mathcal{R}_{\pi}
has the same formula as in Proposition
4.6
with the normal bundle of the codimension two stratum calculated in
[
23
]
. Proposition
9.3
and Proposition
9.9
applied to the inductive structure in Section
9.3
give the result.
∎

Theorem
1.6
coincides with
[
32
, Theorem 1.1]
over
\mathcal{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. After pulling back Theorem
1.6
along the unit section, we recover
[
23
, Theorem 1.1]
.

For more degenerate families
\pi:\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}\to\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{g}
, such as the second Voronoi compactification, the singularities of
\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{g}}\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{g}
get more complicated, and
\delta
-regularity breaks down. It remains an interesting question whether we can generalize our construction to such degenerate abelian schemes.
